## assistant — 18:14

# Plan — pilot corpus of 5 documents

Purpose of this batch: exercise **every hard aspect at least once** so the ingestion pipeline meets all of them early, and produce the ground truth the eval harness will need. Scaling to 50 later is then a content exercise, not a capability one.

## Subject matter

A fictional company — **Aurora Home**, smart-home thermostats and sensors. Entirely invented (no real brand names), which keeps the docs safe to ship in the deliverable. It naturally produces what the corpus needs: spec tables, warranty/returns matrices, wiring diagrams, energy-usage charts, and a plausible reason for EN/DE bilingual documentation.

## Coverage matrix

| # | Doc | Lang | Format | Pages | Hard aspects it carries |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Installation & Setup Guide | EN | PDF | ~14 | **Multi-column** section · **ruled table spanning a page break** · wiring **diagram, image-only, captioned** · deep heading hierarchy, long enough to stress chunking |
| 2 | Technische Daten & Fehlerbehebung | DE | PDF | ~8 | **Unruled table** (spec list, no borders) · battery-life **chart, image-only, no caption** → exercises the generated-caption path · numbers **only** readable inside the image (OCR) |
| 3 | Warranty, Returns & Shipping | EN | DOCX | ~5 | **Ruled table with merged cells** · figures **with captions** · values that appear **nowhere but the table** |
| 4 | Garantie, Rückgabe & Versand | DE | DOCX | ~5 | **Cross-lingual overlap** — deliberately mirrors most of doc 3, giving the ~20% bilingual content and EN↔DE retrieval pairs |
| 5 | Support FAQ | EN + DE | HTML | ~3 | **HTML format** · CSS **multi-column** · second **uncaptioned chart** · content that only answers when **combined with doc 1** (multi-hop) |

Balance: 2 EN, 2 DE, 1 mixed (~50/50); PDF ×2, DOCX ×2, HTML ×1; ~35 pages total.

## Golden Q&A set, generated in the same run

`corpus/golden_qa.json`, each entry carrying question, language, expected answer, source document + section/element, and type — the ground truth for recall@k and MRR:

- **table-only** (returns window from doc 3; a spec from doc 2's unruled table)
- **image-only** (a value legible only in doc 2's chart; a detail only in doc 1's diagram)
- **cross-lingual** (German question answered by the English doc 3, via the doc 3/4 overlap)
- **multi-hop** (doc 5 + doc 1)
- **unanswerable** (a plausible question about something deliberately absent — for testing that we say "not in the documentation" rather than inventing)

## Tooling

`scripts/generate_corpus.py`, with per-document content modules. ReportLab for PDF (pure Python, precise control over columns and page-break tables), `python-docx` for DOCX, plain templates for HTML, matplotlib for the chart images. All in a separate `corpus` dependency group — the app itself never imports them.

**Deterministic output** (fixed seed, fixed dates), so regenerating produces byte-identical files. That matters beyond tidiness: the incremental-ingestion manifest is content-hash based, so stable hashes let us later prove re-ingesting a batch is a no-op.

## Decisions

1. **Domain** — Aurora Home smart-home, or do you want a different subject (SaaS, insurance, e-commerce)?
2. **Commit the generated files, or gitignore and regenerate?** I'd **commit** them: the deliverable is a zip and a reviewer running `docker compose up` should have documents to ingest without running a generator first. Cost is ~35 pages of binaries in git.
3. **Dependencies** — reportlab + python-docx + matplotlib as a `corpus` group. Any objection to matplotlib specifically (it's the heaviest)?
4. **Content authored by hand in the modules**, rather than templated filler — better prose makes the golden set meaningful, at the cost of the later 50 needing more of the same. Agreed?

One thing I'd flag now: **doc 1 at ~14 pages of genuinely useful prose is the bulk of the effort here.** If you'd rather see the machinery working first, I can generate all 5 with shorter bodies and thicken doc 1 afterwards — say if you prefer that.

{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/pdf_kit.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Shared ReportLab scaffolding for the PDF documents.\n\nProvides the two things the corpus needs and a plain PDF writer would not give:\na **two-column** page template, and tables that **split across a page break**\nwith their header repeated (requirements.md \u00a74).\n\"\"\"\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom reportlab import rl_config\n\n# Fixed dates and no random document IDs, so regenerating produces byte-identical\n# PDFs. Content hashes then stay stable for the incremental-ingestion manifest.\nrl_config.invariant = 1\n\nfrom reportlab.lib import colors  # noqa: E402\nfrom reportlab.lib.enums import TA_JUSTIFY  # noqa: E402\nfrom reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4  # noqa: E402\nfrom reportlab.lib.styles import ParagraphStyle, getSampleStyleSheet  # noqa: E402\nfrom reportlab.lib.units import mm  # noqa: E402\nfrom reportlab.platypus import (  # noqa: E402\n    BaseDocTemplate,\n    Frame,\n    Image,\n    LongTable,\n    PageTemplate,\n    Paragraph,\n    Spacer,\n    Table,\n    TableStyle,\n)\n\nMARGIN = 20 * mm\nGUTTER = 8 * mm\n\n\ndef styles() -> dict[str, ParagraphStyle]:\n    base = getSampleStyleSheet()\n    return {\n        \"title\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraTitle\", parent=base[\"Title\"], fontSize=22, leading=26, spaceAfter=6\n        ),\n        \"subtitle\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraSubtitle\",\n            parent=base[\"Normal\"],\n            fontSize=11,\n            textColor=colors.HexColor(\"#555555\"),\n            spaceAfter=18,\n        ),\n        \"h1\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraH1\", parent=base[\"Heading1\"], fontSize=15, leading=19, spaceBefore=14\n        ),\n        \"h2\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraH2\", parent=base[\"Heading2\"], fontSize=12.5, leading=16, spaceBefore=10\n        ),\n        \"body\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraBody\",\n            parent=base[\"BodyText\"],\n            fontSize=10,\n            leading=14.5,\n            alignment=TA_JUSTIFY,\n            spaceAfter=7,\n        ),\n        \"bullet\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraBullet\", parent=base[\"BodyText\"], fontSize=10, leading=14.5, leftIndent=10\n        ),\n        \"caption\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraCaption\",\n            parent=base[\"Normal\"],\n            fontSize=9,\n            leading=12,\n            textColor=colors.HexColor(\"#444444\"),\n            spaceBefore=4,\n            spaceAfter=12,\n        ),\n        \"note\": ParagraphStyle(\n            \"AuroraNote\",\n            parent=base[\"BodyText\"],\n            fontSize=9.5,\n            leading=13,\n            leftIndent=8,\n            textColor=colors.HexColor(\"#333333\"),\n            backColor=colors.HexColor(\"#f4f1ea\"),\n            borderPadding=6,\n            spaceAfter=10,\n        ),\n    }\n\n\ndef ruled_table(data: list[list], widths: list[float], repeat_header: bool = True) -> Table:\n    \"\"\"Bordered table. `LongTable` so it splits across pages, header repeated.\"\"\"\n    table = LongTable(data, colWidths=widths, repeatRows=1 if repeat_header else 0)\n    table.setStyle(\n        TableStyle(\n            [\n                (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, colors.HexColor(\"#999999\")),\n                (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), colors.HexColor(\"#e8e3d8\")),\n                (\"FONTNAME\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), \"Helvetica-Bold\"),\n                (\"FONTSIZE\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 8.5),\n                (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"TOP\"),\n                (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 4),\n                (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 4),\n            ]\n        )\n    )\n    return table\n\n\ndef unruled_table(data: list[list], widths: list[float]) -> Table:\n    \"\"\"No borders at all \u2014 alignment is the only cue that it is a table.\n\n    Deliberately hard: a layout parser has to infer the structure rather than\n    read ruling lines.\n    \"\"\"\n    table = Table(data, colWidths=widths)\n    table.setStyle(\n        TableStyle(\n            [\n                (\"FONTSIZE\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 9.5),\n                (\"FONTNAME\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), \"Helvetica-Bold\"),\n                (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"TOP\"),\n                (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 3),\n                (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 3),\n                (\"LEFTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0),\n            ]\n        )\n    )\n    return table\n\n\ndef figure(path: Path, width: float, caption: str | None, style: ParagraphStyle) -> list:\n    \"\"\"A figure, optionally captioned.\n\n    Passing `caption=None` is the point of several documents: Docling finds no\n    caption, so the generated-description path has to produce one.\n    \"\"\"\n    image = Image(str(path))\n    ratio = image.imageHeight / image.imageWidth\n    image.drawWidth = width\n    image.drawHeight = width * ratio\n    flowables: list = [Spacer(1, 6), image]\n    if caption is not None:\n        flowables.append(Paragraph(caption, style))\n    else:\n        flowables.append(Spacer(1, 12))\n    return flowables\n\n\ndef build_pdf(path: Path, title: str, author: str, story_fn: Callable[[dict, float], list]) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"One- and two-column page templates; the story switches with\n    `NextPageTemplate` + `PageBreak`.\"\"\"\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    page_width, page_height = A4\n    usable = page_width - 2 * MARGIN\n    column = (usable - GUTTER) / 2\n\n    doc = BaseDocTemplate(\n        str(path),\n        pagesize=A4,\n        leftMargin=MARGIN,\n        rightMargin=MARGIN,\n        topMargin=MARGIN,\n        bottomMargin=MARGIN,\n        title=title,\n        author=author,\n    )\n    one = Frame(MARGIN, MARGIN, usable, page_height - 2 * MARGIN, id=\"single\")\n    left = Frame(MARGIN, MARGIN, column, page_height - 2 * MARGIN, id=\"left\")\n    right = Frame(\n        MARGIN + column + GUTTER, MARGIN, column, page_height - 2 * MARGIN, id=\"right\"\n    )\n    doc.addPageTemplates(\n        [\n            PageTemplate(id=\"OneCol\", frames=[one]),\n            PageTemplate(id=\"TwoCol\", frames=[left, right]),\n        ]\n    )\n    doc.build(story_fn(styles(), usable))\n    return path\n"
}

      [\"Combi boiler, OpenTherm\", \"Modulating\", \"Verified\", \"Not required\", \"Modulation requires firmware 4.1 or later\"],\n        [\"System boiler with cylinder\", \"Volt-free contact\", [\"Air-source heat pump, on/off\", \"Volt-free contact\", \"Verified\", \"Not required\", \"Minimum cycle time must be set to 15 minutes\"],\n        [\"Air-source heat pump, modulating\", \"Proprietary bus\", \"Not supported\", \"\u2014\", \"No open control interface\"],\n        [\"Ground-source heat pump\", \"Volt-free contact\", \"Verified\", \"Required\", \"Interface module isolates the control circuit\"],\n        [\"Electric panel heaters\", \"Direct switching\", \"Not supported\", \"\u2014\", \"Load exceeds the T3 relay rating\"],\n        [\"Storage heaters\", \"Time-of-use relay\", \"Not supported\", \"\u2014\", \"Charge control is outside the T3's remit\"],\n        [\"Biomass boiler\", \"Volt-free contact\", \"Verified\", \"Required\", \"Interface module required for safety interlock\"],\n        [\"Oil boiler, on/off\", \"Volt-free contact\", \"Verified\", \"Not required\", \"Same wiring as a heat-only boiler\"],\n        [\"Warm-air unit\", \"Volt-free contact\", \"Verified\", \"Not required\", \"Fan delay configured on the unit itself\"],\n        [\"Solar thermal, supplementary\", \"Monitoring only\", \"Verified\", \"Not required\", [\"Ventilation with heat recovery\", \"Volt-free contact\", \"Verified\", \"Required\", \"Boost function mapped to a schedule\"],\n    ]\n    widths = [usable * w for w in (0.24, 0.17, 0.12, 0.17, 0.30)]\n    story.append(pdf_kit.ruled_table([header, *rows], widths))\n    story.append(Spacer(1, 10))\n    story.append(\n        _p(\n            \"Table 1: Verified heating system types for firmware 4.2.1. The table continues \"\n            \"across the page break; the header row repeats.\",\n            s[\"caption\"],\n        )\n    )\n\n    story += [\n        _p(\"7. Capacity limits\", s[\"h1\"]),\n        _p(\n            \"A single H2 hub supports a maximum of 64 paired devices, counting thermostats, \"\n            \"sensors and accessories together. Properties needing more capacity add a second \"\n            \"hub; the app presents multiple hubs as one property, and automations may span \"\n            \"them. Each hub maintains its own mesh, and a device belongs to exactly one hub.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"In practice the radio mesh becomes the limiting factor before the device count \"\n            \"does. Above roughly forty devices, Aurora Home recommends at least three \"\n            \"mains-powered repeaters spread across the property.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\"8. Firmware updates\", s[\"h1\"]),\n        _p(\n            \"The hub checks for firmware nightly and stages updates for connected devices. \"\n            \"Thermostats update in place and are unavailable for roughly ninety seconds. \"\n            \"Sensors update opportunistically when they next report, so a full property can \"\n            \"take several days to converge \u2014 this is expected and needs no intervention.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"Updates cannot be rolled back. If an update introduces a problem in your specific \"\n            \"installation, contact support rather than attempting to reflash a device.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\"9. Decommissioning\", s[\"h1\"]),\n        _p(\n            \"Remove devices from the app before removing them from the wall. A device removed \"\n            \"physically but left paired continues to occupy a slot against the 64-device limit \"\n            \"and will be reported as unreachable. If you have already removed the hardware, the \"\n            \"app offers a forced removal that clears the pairing from the hub's side.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"Factory-resetting the hub erases every pairing, all schedules and all automation \"\n            \"history. There is no export, so record any complex schedule before resetting.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n    ]\n    return story\n\n\ndef build(out_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    return pdf_kit.build_pdf(\n        out_dir / \"aurora-installation-guide-en.pdf\", TITLE, \"Aurora Home\", _story\n    )\n"
}

In \"\n            \"beheizten Innenr\u00e4umen erreichen die Ger\u00e4te regelm\u00e4\u00dfig mehrere Jahre; in \"\n            \"unbeheizten Nebenr\u00e4umen, Garagen und Winterg\u00e4rten f\u00e4llt die Kapazit\u00e4t deutlich \"\n            \"schneller ab. Die Chemie der Lithium-Knopfzelle ist daf\u00fcr verantwortlich, nicht \"\n            \"eine erh\u00f6hte Sendeleistung des Sensors.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"Der folgende Verlauf zeigt die gemessene Restkapazit\u00e4t \u00fcber die Betriebsdauer bei \"\n            \"drei Umgebungstemperaturen.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n    ]\n\n    # Deliberately no caption: Docling finds none, so a description must be generated.\n    story += pdf_kit.figure(images / \"battery-retention.png\", usable * 0.78, None, s[\"caption\"])\n\n    story += [\n        _p(\n            \"Planen Sie den Batteriewechsel bei kalt montierten Sensoren entsprechend fr\u00fcher \"\n            \"ein. Fehlerbehebung\", s[\"h1\"]),\n        _p(\"4.1 Ger\u00e4t wird als nicht erreichbar angezeigt\", s[\"h2\"]),\n        _p(\n            \"Pr\u00fcfen Sie zuerst, ob das Ger\u00e4t physisch vorhanden und die Batterie eingelegt ist. \"\n            \"Bleibt der Status bestehen, bewegen Sie ein netzbetriebenes Ger\u00e4t n\u00e4her an den \"\n            \"Sensor: In den meisten F\u00e4llen ist die Funkstrecke und nicht das Ger\u00e4t die \"\n            \"Ursache. {
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/docx_kit.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Shared python-docx scaffolding.\"\"\"\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom docx import Document\nfrom docx.enum.text import WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH\nfrom docx.shared import Inches, Pt, RGBColor\n\n# Fixed so regenerating does not churn the document metadata.\nFIXED_TIMESTAMP = datetime(2026, 1, 15, 9, 0, 0)\n\n\ndef new_document(title: str, subtitle: str) -> Document:\n    document = Document()\n    document.core_properties.title = title\n    document.core_properties.author = \"Aurora Home\"\n    document.core_properties.created = FIXED_TIMESTAMP\n    document.core_properties.modified = FIXED_TIMESTAMP\n\n    document.add_heading(title, level=0)\n    lead = document.add_paragraph(subtitle)\n    lead.runs[0].italic = True\n    lead.runs[0].font.color.rgb = RGBColor(0x55, 0x55, 0x55)\n    return document\n\n\ndef add_table(document: Document, header: list[str], rows: list[list[str]]):\n    \"\"\"Ruled table \u2014 `Table Grid` gives every cell a visible border.\"\"\"\n    table = document.add_table(rows=1, cols=len(header))\n    table.style = \"Table Grid\"\n    for cell, text in zip(table.rows[0].cells, header, strict=True):\n        cell.text = \"\"\n        run = cell.paragraphs[0].add_run(text)\n        run.bold = True\n    for row in rows:\n        cells = table.add_row().cells\n        for cell, text in zip(cells, row, strict=True):\n            cell.text = text\n    return table\n\n\ndef add_caption(document: Document, text: str) -> None:\n    paragraph = document.add_paragraph()\n    run = paragraph.add_run(text)\n    run.italic = True\n    run.font.size = Pt(9)\n    run.font.color.rgb = RGBColor(0x44, 0x44, 0x44)\n    paragraph.alignment = WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER\n\n\ndef add_figure(document: Document, image: Path, caption: str | None) -> None:\n    document.add_picture(str(image), width=Inches(5.4))\n    document.paragraphs[-1].alignment = WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER\n    if caption is not None:\n        add_caption(document, caption)\n\n\ndef save(document: Document, path: Path) -> Path:\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    document.save(str(path))\n    return path\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"Doc 3 \u2014 Warranty, Returns and Shipping (EN, DOCX).\n\nCarries: ruled tables with **merged cells**, a captioned figure, and several\nvalues that exist **only in a table** \u2014 the returns window, the restocking fee\nand the shipping times are never restated in prose.\n\nDoc 4 is the German counterpart of this document: together they provide the\ncross-lingual overlap required by requirements.md \u00a74.\n\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom scripts.corpus import docx_kit\n\nTITLE = \"Aurora Home \u2014 Warranty, Returns and Shipping\"\nSUBTITLE = \"Consumer and business customers \u00b7 valid from 1 February 2026 \u00b7 version 3\"\n\n\ndef build(out_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    document = docx_kit.new_document(TITLE, SUBTITLE)\n\n    document.add_heading(\"1. Scope\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"This policy applies to Aurora Home hardware bought directly from Aurora Home or from \"\n        \"an authorised reseller in the European Union, the United Kingdom and Switzerland. \"\n        \"Hardware bought elsewhere is covered by the policy of the market it was sold in. \"\n        \"Software and cloud services are covered by the separate terms of service.\"\n    )\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Nothing in this policy limits your statutory rights. Where national law grants you a \"\n        \"longer period or a stronger remedy than the terms below, national law prevails.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"2. Warranty\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Every Aurora Home device carries a manufacturer's warranty against defects in \"\n        \"materials and workmanship, running from the date on your proof of purchase. \"\n        \"Registering a device extends its warranty at no cost, provided registration happens \"\n        \"within the window shown below.\"\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_table(\n        document,\n        [\"Device\", \"Standard warranty\", \"Extended warranty\", \"Registration window\"],\n        [\n            [\"Thermostat T3\", \"24 months\", \"36 months\", \"60 days from purchase\"],\n            [\"Sensor S1\", \"24 months\", \"36 months\", \"60 days from purchase\"],\n            [\"Hub H2\", \"24 months\", \"36 months\", \"60 days from purchase\"],\n            [\"Interface module AH-IM1\", \"12 months\", \"not available\", \"\u2014\"],\n            [\"Power adapter, cables\", \"12 months\", \"not available\", \"\u2014\"],\n        ],\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_caption(document, \"Table 1: Warranty terms by device.\")\n\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"The warranty covers repair or replacement at Aurora Home's discretion. It does not \"\n        \"cover accidental damage, damage caused by installation contrary to the installation \"\n        \"guide, cosmetic wear, or consumable parts. Batteries are consumables.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"2.1 Making a warranty claim\", level=2)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Contact support with the device identifier and your proof of purchase. Where a claim \"\n        \"is accepted, Aurora Home issues a prepaid return label. Replacement units ship under \"\n        \"the same delivery terms as new orders, and the replacement inherits the remaining \"\n        \"warranty of the original device rather than starting a new term.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"3. Returns\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Returns are handled separately from warranty claims: a return is for hardware you no \"\n        \"longer want, a warranty claim is for hardware that has failed. The windows and fees \"\n        \"below apply to returns.\"\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_table(\n        document,\n        [\"Customer type\", \"Return window\", \"Condition\", \"Restocking fee\", \"Refund method\"],\n        [\n            [\"Consumer\", \"30 days\", \"Unopened\", \"None\", \"Original payment method\"],\n            [\"Consumer\", \"30 days\", \"Opened, complete\", \"None\", \"Original payment method\"],\n            [\"Consumer\", \"30 days\", \"Opened, incomplete\", \"25%\", \"Original payment method\"],\n            [\"Business\", \"14 days\", \"Unopened\", \"None\", \"Credit note\"],\n            [\"Business\", \"14 days\", \"Opened, complete\", \"15%\", \"Credit note\"],\n            [\"Business\", \"14 days\", \"Opened, incomplete\", \"30%\", \"Credit note\"],\n            [\"Installer partner\", \"45 days\", \"Any\", \"None\", \"Account credit\"],\n        ],\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_caption(document, \"Table 2: Return windows and restocking fees.\")\n\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Return shipping is at your own cost unless the return follows an accepted warranty \"\n        \"claim or a delivery error on our side. Refunds are issued within ten working days of \"\n        \"the returned hardware arriving at our warehouse.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"4. Shipping\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Orders placed before 14:00 CET on a working day are dispatched the same day. Orders \"\n        \"placed later, or at a weekend, are dispatched the next working day.\"\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_table(\n        document,\n        [\"Destination\", \"Standard delivery\", \"Express delivery\", \"Free over\"],\n        [\n            [\"Germany\", \"2\u20133 working days\", \"next working day\", \"\u20ac49\"],\n            [\"Austria, Netherlands, Belgium\", \"3\u20134 working days\", \"2 working days\", \"\u20ac59\"],\n            [\"Rest of the European Union\", \"4\u20136 working days\", \"3 working days\", \"\u20ac79\"],\n            [\"Switzerland\", \"5\u20137 working days\", \"3 working days\", \"not available\"],\n            [\"United Kingdom\", \"5\u20137 working days\", \"3 working days\", \"not available\"],\n        ],\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_caption(document, \"Table 3: Delivery times and free-shipping thresholds.\")\n\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Deliveries outside the European Union may attract customs duty and import VAT, which \"\n        \"are payable by the recipient and are not included in the prices shown at checkout.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"5. Contact\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Written enquiries about this policy should go to the support address in the app. \"\n        \"Please quote your order number: without it we cannot locate a purchase, and a warranty \"\n        \"claim cannot be opened on a device we cannot tie to a sale.\"\n    )\n\n    return docx_kit.save(document, out_dir / \"aurora-warranty-returns-shipping-en.docx\")\n"

Februar 2026 \u00b7 Version 3\"\n\n\ndef build(out_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    document = docx_kit.new_document(TITLE, SUBTITLE)\n\n    document.add_heading(\"1. Geltungsbereich\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Diese Richtlinie gilt f\u00fcr Aurora-Home-Hardware, die direkt bei Aurora Home oder bei \"\n        \"einem autorisierten Fachh\u00e4ndler in der Europ\u00e4ischen Union, im Vereinigten K\u00f6nigreich \"\n        \"und in der Schweiz gekauft wurde. F\u00fcr Hardware aus anderen M\u00e4rkten gilt die dortige \"\n        \"Richtlinie. Software und Cloud-Dienste unterliegen den gesonderten Nutzungsbedingungen.\"\n    )\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Ihre gesetzlichen Rechte bleiben unber\u00fchrt. Sieht das nationale Recht eine l\u00e4ngere \"\n        \"Frist oder einen weitergehenden Anspruch vor, gilt das nationale Recht.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"2. Garantie\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"F\u00fcr jedes Aurora-Home-Ger\u00e4t gilt eine Herstellergarantie auf Material- und \"\n        \"Verarbeitungsfehler ab dem Datum des Kaufbelegs. Wer sein Ger\u00e4t registriert, erh\u00e4lt \"\n        \"die verl\u00e4ngerte Garantie kostenlos \u2014 sofern die Registrierung innerhalb der unten \"\n        \"genannten Frist erfolgt.\"\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_table(\n        document,\n        [\"Ger\u00e4t\", \"Standardgarantie\", \"Verl\u00e4ngerte Garantie\", \"Registrierungsfrist\"],\n        [\n            [\"Thermostat T3\", \"24 Monate\", \"36 Monate\", \"60 Tage ab Kauf\"],\n            [\"Sensor S1\", \"24 Monate\", \"36 Monate\", \"60 Tage ab Kauf\"],\n            [\"Hub H2\", \"24 Monate\", \"36 Monate\", \"60 Tage ab Kauf\"],\n            [\"Schnittstellenmodul AH-IM1\", \"12 Monate\", \"nicht verf\u00fcgbar\", \"\u2014\"],\n            [\"Netzteil, Kabel\", \"12 Monate\", \"nicht verf\u00fcgbar\", \"\u2014\"],\n        ],\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_caption(document, \"Tabelle 1: Garantiebedingungen nach Ger\u00e4t.\")\n\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Die Garantie umfasst nach Wahl von Aurora Home die Reparatur oder den Austausch. Nicht \"\n        \"abgedeckt sind Unfallsch\u00e4den, Sch\u00e4den durch eine Installation entgegen dem \"\n        \"Installationshandbuch, optischer Verschlei\u00df sowie Verbrauchsteile. Batterien gelten \"\n        \"als Verbrauchsteil.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"2.1 Garantiefall melden\", level=2)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Wenden Sie sich mit der Ger\u00e4tekennung und dem Kaufbeleg an den Support. Wird der \"\n        \"Garantiefall anerkannt, stellt Aurora Home ein vorfrankiertes R\u00fccksendeetikett bereit. \"\n        \"F\u00fcr Austauschger\u00e4te gelten dieselben Lieferbedingungen wie f\u00fcr Neubestellungen; die \"\n        \"Restlaufzeit der urspr\u00fcnglichen Garantie geht auf das Austauschger\u00e4t \u00fcber, es beginnt \"\n        \"keine neue Frist.\"\n    )\n\n    # German-only section: the overlap between doc 3 and doc 4 is deliberately partial.\n    document.add_heading(\"2.2 Reparatur vor Ort\", level=2)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"In Deutschland und \u00d6sterreich bietet Aurora Home f\u00fcr gewerbliche Installationen ab \"\n        \"zwanzig Ger\u00e4ten eine Vor-Ort-Reparatur an. Der Einsatz erfolgt innerhalb von f\u00fcnf \"\n        \"Werktagen nach Anerkennung des Garantiefalls und ist auf Objekte mit einem g\u00fcltigen \"\n        \"Wartungsvertrag beschr\u00e4nkt. F\u00fcr Privatkundinnen und Privatkunden ist die \"\n        \"Vor-Ort-Reparatur nicht verf\u00fcgbar.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"3. R\u00fcckgabe\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"R\u00fcckgabe und Garantiefall sind zu unterscheiden: Eine R\u00fcckgabe betrifft Hardware, die \"\n        \"Sie nicht behalten m\u00f6chten, ein Garantiefall betrifft defekte Hardware. F\u00fcr R\u00fcckgaben \"\n        \"gelten die folgenden Fristen und Geb\u00fchren.\"\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_table(\n        document,\n        [\"Kundengruppe\", \"R\u00fcckgabefrist\", \"Zustand\", \"Wiedereinlagerungsgeb\u00fchr\", \"Erstattung\"],\n        [\n            [\"Privatkunden\", \"30 Tage\", \"unge\u00f6ffnet\", \"keine\", \"urspr\u00fcngliches Zahlungsmittel\"],\n            [\"Privatkunden\", \"30 Tage\", \"ge\u00f6ffnet, vollst\u00e4ndig\", \"keine\", \"urspr\u00fcngliches Zahlungsmittel\"],\n            [\"Privatkunden\", \"30 Tage\", \"ge\u00f6ffnet, unvollst\u00e4ndig\", \"25 %\", \"urspr\u00fcngliches Zahlungsmittel\"],\n            [\"Gesch\u00e4ftskunden\", \"14 Tage\", \"unge\u00f6ffnet\", \"keine\", \"Gutschrift\"],\n            [\"Gesch\u00e4ftskunden\", \"14 Tage\", \"ge\u00f6ffnet, vollst\u00e4ndig\", \"15 %\", \"Gutschrift\"],\n            [\"Gesch\u00e4ftskunden\", \"14 Tage\", \"ge\u00f6ffnet, unvollst\u00e4ndig\", \"30 %\", \"Gutschrift\"],\n            [\"Installationspartner\", \"45 Tage\", \"beliebig\", \"keine\", \"Kontoguthaben\"],\n        ],\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_caption(document, \"Tabelle 2: R\u00fcckgabefristen und Wiedereinlagerungsgeb\u00fchren.\")\n\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Die R\u00fccksendekosten tragen Sie selbst, sofern die R\u00fccksendung nicht auf einen \"\n        \"anerkannten Garantiefall oder einen Lieferfehler unsererseits zur\u00fcckgeht. Die \"\n        \"Erstattung erfolgt innerhalb von zehn Werktagen nach Eingang der Ware in unserem Lager.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"4. Versand\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Bestellungen, die an einem Werktag vor 14:00 Uhr MEZ eingehen, werden am selben Tag \"\n        \"versandt. Sp\u00e4tere Bestellungen sowie Bestellungen am Wochenende gehen am n\u00e4chsten \"\n        \"Werktag raus.\"\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_table(\n        document,\n        [\"Zielgebiet\", \"Standardversand\", \"Expressversand\", \"Versandfrei ab\"],\n        [\n            [\"Deutschland\", \"2\u20133 Werktage\", \"n\u00e4chster Werktag\", \"49 \u20ac\"],\n            [\"\u00d6sterreich, Niederlande, Belgien\", \"3\u20134 Werktage\", \"2 Werktage\", \"59 \u20ac\"],\n            [\"\u00dcbrige Europ\u00e4ische Union\", \"4\u20136 Werktage\", \"3 Werktage\", \"79 \u20ac\"],\n            [\"Schweiz\", \"5\u20137 Werktage\", \"3 Werktage\", \"nicht verf\u00fcgbar\"],\n            [\"Vereinigtes K\u00f6nigreich\", \"5\u20137 Werktage\", \"3 Werktage\", \"nicht verf\u00fcgbar\"],\n        ],\n    )\n    docx_kit.add_caption(document, \"Tabelle 3: Lieferzeiten und Versandkostengrenzen.\")\n\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Bei Lieferungen au\u00dferhalb der Europ\u00e4ischen Union k\u00f6nnen Zoll und Einfuhrumsatzsteuer \"\n        \"anfallen. Diese tr\u00e4gt die empfangende Person; sie sind in den angezeigten Preisen \"\n        \"nicht enthalten.\"\n    )\n\n    document.add_heading(\"5. Kontakt\", level=1)\n    document.add_paragraph(\n        \"Schriftliche Anfragen zu dieser Richtlinie richten Sie bitte an die in der App \"\n        \"hinterlegte Support-Adresse. Geben Sie dabei Ihre Bestellnummer an: Ohne sie l\u00e4sst \"\n        \"sich ein Kauf nicht zuordnen und ein Garantiefall nicht er\u00f6ffnen.\"\n    )\n\n    return docx_kit.save(document, out_dir / \"aurora-garantie-ruckgabe-versand-de.docx\")\n"
}

Doc 5 — the bilingual HTML FAQ with CSS columns, an uncaptioned chart, and the multi-hop hook:

<details><summary>tool_use: Write — /home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/doc5_faq.py</summary>

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/doc5_faq.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Doc 5 \u2014 Support FAQ (EN + DE, HTML).\n\nCarries: the HTML format, a CSS **multi-column** section, an **uncaptioned**\nchart, and the multi-hop hook \u2014 the second-hub statement here only answers a\ncapacity question when combined with the 64-device limit in doc 1.\n\nBoth languages live in one page, as a real FAQ often does.\n\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nTITLE = \"Aurora Home \u2014 Support FAQ\"\n\n_HTML = \"\"\"<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n  <head>\n    <meta charset=\"utf-8\" />\n    <title>Aurora Home \u2014 Support FAQ</title>\n    <style>\n      body { font-family: Georgia, serif; max-width: 52rem; margin: 2rem auto; color: #1b1b1b; }\n      h1 { font-size: 1.9rem; }\n      h2 { font-size: 1.3rem; margin-top: 2rem; border-bottom: 1px solid #ddd; }\n      h3 { font-size: 1.05rem; margin-bottom: .2rem; }\n      .lede { color: #555; font-style: italic; }\n      .two-column { column-count: 2; column-gap: 2.5rem; }\n      .two-column h3 { break-after: avoid; }\n      figure { margin: 1.5rem 0; }\n      figure img { width: 100%; max-width: 34rem; }\n      table { border-collapse: collapse; margin: 1rem 0; }\n      th, td { border: 1px solid #999; padding: .35rem .6rem; text-align: left; }\n      th { background: #e8e3d8; }\n    </style>\n  </head>\n  <body>\n    <h1>Aurora Home \u2014 Support FAQ</h1>\n    <p class=\"lede\">Frequently asked questions \u00b7 H\u00e4ufige Fragen \u00b7 updated February 2026</p>\n\n    <h2>Getting help</h2>\n    <p>\n      Support is staffed Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 18:00 CET, excluding public holidays in\n      Berlin. Messages sent outside those hours are answered on the next working day. There is\n      no telephone line: every enquiry goes through the app so that the device history travels\n      with the ticket.\n    </p>\n\n    <figure>\n      <img src=\"images/support-volume.png\" alt=\"\" />\n    </figure>\n\n    <p>\n      Response times vary through the week. If your question is not urgent, the middle of the\n      week is usually quieter than either end of it.\n    </p>\n\n    <h2>Accounts and property setup</h2>\n    <div class=\"two-column\">\n      <h3>Can I have more than one property on one account?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Yes. An account can hold any number of properties, each with its own hubs, zones and\n        schedules. Switching property in the app switches the whole context, so a schedule\n        edited in one property never affects another.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>What happens if I need more capacity?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Add a second hub. Each additional hub adds its own full device capacity to the\n        property, and the app continues to present the property as a single system. Devices\n        belong to exactly one hub and can be moved between hubs by unpairing and re-pairing.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>Can I share access with someone else?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Yes, per property. A shared user can adjust temperatures and run automations, but\n        cannot add or remove hubs, and cannot see billing information.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>Wie viele Zonen kann ich anlegen?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Die Anzahl der Zonen ist nicht begrenzt. Praktisch begrenzt wird sie durch die Zahl der\n        Thermostate: Jede Zone ben\u00f6tigt genau ein Thermostat, Sensoren k\u00f6nnen beliebig viele\n        hinzukommen.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>Kann ich die App ohne Hub nutzen?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Nein. Ohne Hub lassen sich Ger\u00e4te weder einbinden noch fernsteuern. Ein Thermostat\n        funktioniert dann nur als eigenst\u00e4ndiger Regler direkt an der Wand.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>Was passiert bei einem Internetausfall?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Zeitpl\u00e4ne laufen lokal auf dem Hub weiter. Fernzugriff und Sprachassistenten sind\n        w\u00e4hrend des Ausfalls nicht verf\u00fcgbar, die Heizung wird aber weiter geregelt.\n      </p>\n    </div>\n\n    <h2>Orders and deliveries</h2>\n    <p>\n      Delivery times, return windows and warranty terms are set out in the warranty, returns\n      and shipping policy, which is the authoritative document for all three. This FAQ does not\n      repeat those figures, because a policy that exists in two places eventually disagrees\n      with itself.\n    </p>\n\n    <h3>Where do replacement units ship from?</h3>\n    <p>\n      Replacements ship from the same warehouse as new orders and under the same delivery\n      terms, so the delivery time for your destination applies unchanged.\n    </p>\n\n    <h2>Datenschutz</h2>\n    <p>\n      Temperatur- und Sensordaten werden dem Konto zugeordnet gespeichert, solange das Konto\n      besteht. Beim L\u00f6schen des Kontos werden alle Messwerte innerhalb von 30 Tagen entfernt.\n      Anonymisierte Auswertungen zur Produktverbesserung bleiben davon unber\u00fchrt und lassen\n      keinen R\u00fcckschluss auf einzelne Haushalte zu.\n    </p>\n\n    <h2>Feature requests</h2>\n    <p>\n      Suggestions go through the app's feedback form rather than support tickets. Aurora Home\n      does not commit to timelines for individual requests, and does not maintain a public\n      roadmap.\n    </p>\n  </body>\n</html>\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef build(out_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    path = out_dir / \"aurora-support-faq.html\"\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    path.write_text(_HTML, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    return path\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/golden_qa.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Golden question/answer set, generated alongside the documents.\n\nThis is the ground truth for the eval harness: `source` names the document and\nthe element the answer lives in, which is what makes recall@k and MRR\ncomputable without hand-labelling later.\n\nWritten here rather than derived from the documents on purpose \u2014 a set derived\nfrom the same code that wrote the documents would only prove the code is\nself-consistent.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nQA = [\n    # ---- table-only: the answer exists in a table and nowhere in prose ----\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-001\",\n        \"type\": \"table_only\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"How long do business customers have to return an opened but complete Aurora Home order, and what fee applies?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"14 days, with a 15% restocking fee.\",\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-warranty-returns-shipping-en.docx\", \"element\": \"Table 2\"},\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-002\",\n        \"type\": \"table_only\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"Wie lang ist die Standardgarantie f\u00fcr das Schnittstellenmodul AH-IM1?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"12 Monate; eine verl\u00e4ngerte Garantie ist daf\u00fcr nicht verf\u00fcgbar.\",\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-garantie-ruckgabe-versand-de.docx\", \"element\": \"Tabelle 1\"},\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-003\",\n        \"type\": \"table_only\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"Welche Betriebstemperatur ist f\u00fcr den Sensor S1 angegeben?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"\u221215 \u00b0C bis 50 \u00b0C.\",\n        \"source\": {\n            \"document\": \"aurora-technische-daten-de.pdf\",\n            \"element\": \"unruled specification table, section 2.2\",\n        },\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-004\",\n        \"type\": \"table_only\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"Does the T3 support electric underfloor heating?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"No \u2014 it is listed as not supported because the load exceeds the T3 relay rating.\",\n        \"source\": {\n            \"document\": \"aurora-installation-guide-en.pdf\",\n            \"element\": \"Table 1, compatibility matrix (spans a page break)\",\n        },\n    },\n    # ---- image-only: the answer is legible only inside a figure ----\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-005\",\n        \"type\": \"image_only\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"Wie viel Restkapazit\u00e4t hat die Batterie des S1 nach 15 Monaten bei \u221210 \u00b0C?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"Etwa 40 %.\",\n        \"source\": {\n            \"document\": \"aurora-technische-daten-de.pdf\",\n            \"element\": \"uncaptioned battery retention chart, section 3\",\n        },\n        \"notes\": \"Value appears only in the chart; the prose says temperature matters but gives no figures.\",\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-006\",\n        \"type\": \"image_only\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"Which terminal must be connected for the Aurora T3 to work, and why?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"The C (common) terminal, because the T3 draws continuous power.\",\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-installation-guide-en.pdf\", \"element\": \"Figure 1, wiring diagram\"},\n        \"notes\": \"The C-wire requirement is stated only inside the diagram.\",\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-007\",\n        \"type\": \"image_only\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"Which weekday does Aurora Home support receive the most contacts on?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"Friday, with about 505 contacts.\",\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-support-faq.html\", \"element\": \"uncaptioned support volume chart\"},\n    },\n    # ---- cross-lingual: question language differs from the document that answers best ----\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-008\",\n        \"type\": \"cross_lingual\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"What is the free-shipping threshold for orders delivered within Germany?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"\u20ac49.\",\n        \"source\": {\n            \"document\": \"aurora-garantie-ruckgabe-versand-de.docx\",\n            \"element\": \"Tabelle 3\",\n            \"also_in\": \"aurora-warranty-returns-shipping-en.docx (Table 3)\",\n        },\n        \"notes\": \"English question, answer present in both the German and English policy.\",\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-009\",\n        \"type\": \"cross_lingual\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"Bietet Aurora Home eine Vor-Ort-Reparatur an, und f\u00fcr wen?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": (\n            \"Ja, in Deutschland und \u00d6sterreich f\u00fcr gewerbliche Installationen ab zwanzig \"\n            \"Ger\u00e4ten mit g\u00fcltigem Wartungsvertrag; f\u00fcr Privatkunden nicht.\"\n        ),\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-garantie-ruckgabe-versand-de.docx\", \"element\": \"section 2.2\"},\n        \"notes\": \"German-only section \u2014 the English policy has no counterpart, so the overlap is partial.\",\n    },\n    # ---- multi-hop: needs two documents combined ----\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-010\",\n        \"type\": \"multi_hop\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"If I install two hubs in one property, how many devices can I connect in total?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"128 \u2014 each hub supports 64 devices, and a second hub adds its own full capacity.\",\n        \"source\": {\n            \"documents\": [\"aurora-installation-guide-en.pdf\", \"aurora-support-faq.html\"],\n            \"element\": \"section 7 capacity limit + FAQ 'What happens if I need more capacity?'\",\n        },\n        \"notes\": \"Neither document states 128; the total requires combining both.\",\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-011\",\n        \"type\": \"multi_hop\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"How long will a warranty replacement take to reach me in Austria?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": (\n            \"3\u20134 working days by standard delivery \u2014 replacements ship under the same delivery \"\n            \"terms as new orders.\"\n        ),\n        \"source\": {\n            \"documents\": [\"aurora-support-faq.html\", \"aurora-warranty-returns-shipping-en.docx\"],\n            \"element\": \"FAQ 'Where do replacement units ship from?' + Table 3\",\n        },\n    },\n    # ---- unanswerable: must be declined, not invented ----\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-012\",\n        \"type\": \"unanswerable\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"Does the Aurora T3 work with Matter over Thread?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"Not covered by the documentation \u2014 the assistant should say so rather than guess.\",\n        \"source\": None,\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-013\",\n        \"type\": \"unanswerable\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"Wie hoch ist der monatliche Preis f\u00fcr das Aurora-Home-Cloud-Abonnement?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"In der Dokumentation nicht enthalten \u2014 es darf kein Preis erfunden werden.\",\n        \"source\": None,\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-014\",\n        \"type\": \"unanswerable\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"What is the IP rating of the Aurora outdoor sensor?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"Not covered \u2014 there is no outdoor sensor in the product range.\",\n        \"source\": None,\n        \"notes\": \"False premise: the assistant should not invent a product to answer about.\",\n    },\n    # ---- plain single-hop prose, as a baseline ----\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-015\",\n        \"type\": \"prose\",\n        \"language\": \"en\",\n        \"question\": \"When is Aurora Home support available?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": \"Monday to Friday, 08:00\u201318:00 CET, excluding Berlin public holidays.\",\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-support-faq.html\", \"element\": \"Getting help\"},\n    },\n    {\n        \"id\": \"qa-016\",\n        \"type\": \"prose\",\n        \"language\": \"de\",\n        \"question\": \"Was soll ich tun, wenn ein Ger\u00e4t als nicht erreichbar angezeigt wird?\",\n        \"expected_answer\": (\n            \"Zuerst Ger\u00e4t und Batterie pr\u00fcfen, dann ein netzbetriebenes Ger\u00e4t n\u00e4her an den \"\n            \"Sensor bringen \u2014 meist ist die Funkstrecke die Ursache, nicht das Ger\u00e4t.\"\n        ),\n        \"source\": {\"document\": \"aurora-technische-daten-de.pdf\", \"element\": \"section 4.1\"},\n    },\n]\n\n\ndef write(path: Path) -> Path:\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    path.write_text(json.dumps(QA, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    return path\n\n\ndef summary() -> dict[str, int]:\n    counts: dict[str, int] = {}\n    for entry in QA:\n        counts[entry[\"type\"]] = counts.get(entry[\"type\"], 0) + 1\n    return counts\n"
}

  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/generate_corpus.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Generate the test document corpus and its golden Q&A set.\n\n    uv run python -m scripts.generate_corpus\n\nWrites to `corpus/docs/` and `corpus/golden_qa.json`. Output is committed: the\ndeliverable is a single zip, and a reviewer should have documents to ingest\nwithout running a generator first.\n\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom scripts.corpus import (\n    doc1_installation,\n    doc2_technische_daten,\n    doc3_warranty,\n    doc4_garantie,\n    doc5_faq,\n    golden_qa,\n    images,\n)\n\nCORPUS_DIR = Path(\"corpus\")\nDOCS_DIR = CORPUS_DIR / \"docs\"\nIMAGES_DIR = DOCS_DIR / \"images\"\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    DOCS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    print(\"figures:\")\n    for name, path in images.build_all(IMAGES_DIR).items():\n        print(f\"  {name:16} {path}\")\n\n    builders = [\n        (\"EN \u00b7 PDF  \u00b7 installation\", doc1_installation.build),\n        (\"DE \u00b7 PDF  \u00b7 technical\", doc2_technische_daten.build),\n        (\"EN \u00b7 DOCX \u00b7 warranty\", doc3_warranty.build),\n        (\"DE \u00b7 DOCX \u00b7 garantie\", doc4_garantie.build),\n        (\"EN+DE \u00b7 HTML \u00b7 faq\", doc5_faq.build),\n    ]\n    print(\"documents:\")\n    for label, build in builders:\n        path = build(DOCS_DIR)\n        size_kb = path.stat().st_size / 1024\n        print(f\"  {label:26} {path.name:44} {size_kb:7.1f} KB\")\n\n    qa_path = golden_qa.write(CORPUS_DIR / \"golden_qa.json\")\n    print(f\"golden set: {qa_path} ({len(golden_qa.QA)} questions)\")\n    for kind, count in sorted(golden_qa.summary().items()):\n        print(f\"  {kind:15} {count}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}
```

Three real defects: the table doesn't actually span a page break (so its caption lies), cell text overflows and overlaps because plain-string cells don't wrap, and doc 1 is 5 pages rather than long enough to stress chunking. Fixing all three.

<details><summary>tool_use: Edit — /home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/pdf_kit.py</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/pdf_kit.py",
  "old_string": "def ruled_table(data: list[list], widths: list[float], repeat_header: bool = True) -> Table:\n    \"\"\"Bordered table. `LongTable` so it splits across pages, header repeated.\"\"\"\n    table = LongTable(data, colWidths=widths, repeatRows=1 if repeat_header else 0)\n    table.setStyle(\n        TableStyle(\n            [\n                (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, colors.HexColor(\"#999999\")),\n                (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), colors.HexColor(\"#e8e3d8\")),\n                (\"FONTNAME\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), \"Helvetica-Bold\"),\n                (\"FONTSIZE\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 8.5),\n                (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"TOP\"),\n                (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 4),\n                (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 4),\n            ]\n        )\n    )\n    return table",
  "new_string": "def _wrapped(data: list[list], bold_header: bool) -> list[list]:\n    \"\"\"Cells as Paragraphs so long text wraps.\n\n    Plain strings do not wrap in ReportLab \u2014 they overflow the column and\n    overprint the neighbouring cell.\n    \"\"\"\n    cell = ParagraphStyle(\"Cell\", fontName=\"Helvetica\", fontSize=8.5, leading=10.5)\n    head = ParagraphStyle(\n        \"CellHead\", fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\" if bold_header else \"Helvetica\", fontSize=8.5, leading=10.5\n    )\n    return [\n        [Paragraph(str(text), head if index == 0 else cell) for text in row]\n        for index, row in enumerate(data)\n    ]\n\n\ndef ruled_table(data: list[list], widths: list[float], repeat_header: bool = True) -> Table:\n    \"\"\"Bordered table. `LongTable` so it splits across pages, header repeated.\"\"\"\n    table = LongTable(\n        _wrapped(data, bold_header=True), colWidths=widths, repeatRows=1 if repeat_header else 0\n    )\n    table.setStyle(\n        TableStyle(\n            [\n                (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, colors.HexColor(\"#999999\")),\n                (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), colors.HexColor(\"#e8e3d8\")),\n                (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"TOP\"),\n                (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 4),\n                (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 4),\n            ]\n        )\n    )\n    return table"
}

Commissioning several properties\", s[\"h1\"]),\n        _p(\n            \"Installers commissioning a block of flats or a small estate should treat each \"\n            \"dwelling as a separate property in the app rather than as zones of one large \"\n            \"property. Zones within a property share holiday mode and share the device budget \"\n            \"of their hub, neither of which is what you want across separate households. The \"\n            \"extra work is a few minutes per dwelling at handover and saves an awkward \"\n            \"migration later.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"Name devices for the room rather than the resident. An installer account that retains access after handover is a \"\n            \"support liability: the resident cannot remove it themselves, and Aurora Home will \"\n            \"not remove it on request without the installer's confirmation. Transfer ownership \"\n            \"properly at handover rather than sharing a login.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"Leave the property with at least one schedule in place. Maintenance\", s[\"h1\"]),\n        _p(\n            \"Aurora Home devices need no scheduled servicing. Sensor batteries are the only \"\n            \"consumable, and the app reports a low battery well before the device stops \"\n            \"reporting. Replace with the specified cell type: rechargeable equivalents have a \"\n            \"lower nominal voltage and the low-battery warning will fire almost immediately.\",\n            s[\"body\"],\n        ),\n        _p(\n            \"Clean the thermostat with a dry cloth. DOCX aren't reproducible — python-docx stamps zip entries with the current time. Normalising them:

<details><summary>tool_use: Edit — /home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/docx_kit.py</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/docx_kit.py",
  "old_string": "def save(document: Document, path: Path) -> Path:\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    document.save(str(path))\n    return path",
  "new_string": "def _normalise_zip(path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Rewrite the .docx with fixed zip timestamps and a stable entry order.\n\n    A .docx is a zip, and python-docx stamps each entry with the time of writing\n    \u2014 so two runs produce different bytes even with identical content. Content\n    hashes drive the incremental-ingestion manifest, so that churn matters.\n    \"\"\"\n    fixed = (FIXED_TIMESTAMP.year, FIXED_TIMESTAMP.month, FIXED_TIMESTAMP.day, 9, 0, 0)\n    temporary = path.with_suffix(path.suffix + \".tmp\")\n\n    with zipfile.ZipFile(path) as source:\n        entries = sorted(source.infolist(), key=lambda item: item.filename)\n        payload = [(item, source.read(item.filename)) for item in entries]\n\n    with zipfile.ZipFile(temporary, \"w\", zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as target:\n        for item, data in payload:\n            info = zipfile.ZipInfo(item.filename, date_time=fixed)\n            info.compress_type = zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED\n            info.external_attr = item.external_attr\n            target.writestr(info, data)\n\n    temporary.replace(path)\n\n\ndef save(document: Document, path: Path) -> Path:\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    document.save(str(path))\n    _normalise_zip(path)\n    return path"
}
```

**5 documents + 3 figures + golden set**, in `corpus/`, from `scripts/generate_corpus.py`:

| Document | Lang | Format | Pages | Hard aspects |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| installation-guide | EN | PDF | 7 | two-column section · table spanning a page break, header repeated · captioned wiring diagram |
| technische-daten | DE | PDF | 3 | unruled spec tables · uncaptioned battery chart |
| warranty-returns-shipping | EN | DOCX | ~4 | ruled captioned tables · table-only values |
| garantie-rückgabe-versand | DE | DOCX | ~4 | cross-lingual counterpart, deliberately partial |
| support-faq | EN+DE | HTML | — | CSS multi-column · uncaptioned chart · multi-hop hook |

**I rendered the PDFs and looked at them**, which caught three defects the generator reported no error for:

- **The compatibility table didn't span a page break** — all 18 rows fit on one page, so the caption claiming "the table continues across the page break" was a lie in the document. Extended to 35 rows; it now splits across pages 4→5 with the header repeated, verified in the extracted text.
- **Cell text overflowed and overprinted neighbouring columns** — ReportLab doesn't wrap plain-string cells. All cells are now `Paragraph`s.
- **The wiring diagram clipped its own label** ("24 V AC power" ran under the box border), which matters for an image whose whole purpose is being OCR'd.

**Reproducibility**: PDFs, HTML and PNGs were byte-stable via `rl_config.invariant`, but **DOCX weren't** — python-docx stamps zip entries with the current time, so every run churned the hash that incremental ingestion depends on. Zip entries are now rewritten with fixed timestamps and sorted order; all eight artifacts regenerate identically, and the files still parse.

**Golden set**: 16 questions — 4 table-only, 3 image-only, 2 cross-lingual, 2 multi-hop, 3 unanswerable (one with a false premise), 2 prose — each naming the source document and element. Hand-written rather than derived from the documents, since a derived set would only prove the generator is self-consistent.

**Gaps I recorded rather than papered over** (in `corpus/README.md` and work.md): nothing approaches the 25-page bound (longest is 7), and 2 of 3 figures are uncaptioned — higher than the "most figures captioned" the requirements ask for, so later batches should skew back.

## user — 18:41

would it be cheaper/easier for the rest to generate the files directly instead of generating code to generate the files?

## assistant — 18:42

Good question, and the answer changed once batch 1 existed. Three things worth separating:

**The toolkit is already paid for.** `pdf_kit` / `docx_kit` / `images` are ~300 lines and won't be written again. What made batch 1 expensive was authoring prose, and that cost is identical whichever way the files get produced.

**I can't actually write PDF or DOCX directly** — they're binary. "Directly" really means *authoring in a text format and rendering it*, which is still code, just more generic code.

**The real overhead in the current approach** is the Python syntax wrapped around each paragraph — `_p("...", s["body"]),`. That's maybe 15–20% on top of the prose, and it makes the content harder for you to read or edit than plain text would be.

## What I'd actually change — but for a different reason

The bigger realisation is **what docs 6–50 are for**. Coverage is done: every hard aspect is exercised. The remaining documents exist almost entirely to make retrieval *non-trivial* — with 5 documents, recall@5 is 100% by construction and the golden set can't tell a good retriever from a bad one. They're **distractors**, and distractors need to be plausible and topically adjacent, not artisanal.

That argues for a different split than "code vs direct":

- **Coverage docs (done)** — hand-authored, bespoke rendering. Keep as is.
- **Distractor bulk (6–50)** — content as data plus a generic renderer, with structure varied per document. Much of it can be combinatorial: per-product, per-region and per-release variations of policy pages, release notes, troubleshooting articles. Cheap per document, and genuinely varied.

One caution against going *fully* generic: 45 documents from one renderer share one structural fingerprint, and a corpus that uniform quietly flatters the parser. Keeping a handful of bespoke layouts in the mix is worth the extra effort.

## The question underneath

**Do you need 65 documents at all?** That number is self-imposed — we wrote it into our own requirements; the brief says nothing about corpus size and grades architectural reasoning, not volume. But the retrieval-measurement argument above is real, and it sets a floor: you need enough documents that top-k retrieval is a discriminating test. My instinct is that floor is **~20–30**, not 65, and that the remaining effort is better spent on the eval harness and ingestion than on documents 31–65.

So my recommendation:

1. Switch bulk authoring to **content-as-data + one renderer** (saves the syntax overhead, makes content reviewable).
2. Generate the bulk **combinatorially** as distractors, keeping 3–4 bespoke layouts for variety.
3. **Cut the target from 65 to ~25**, and record the reasoning — "enough documents that recall@k discriminates" is a more defensible number than 65, and it's the kind of decision the brief actually rewards.

Want me to plan that, or keep the 65 target and just switch the authoring format?

**Result**
```
### Corpus

- 1–25 pages per document. 50 initial documents, then a batch of 5, then a batch
  of 10, added on top of the already-indexed set to demonstrate incremental
  ingestion
- Generated by us, not provided. ~50/50 EN/DE, with ~20% of content expressed in
  both languages to exercise cross-lingual retrieval
- **Subject:** *Aurora Home*, an invented smart-home company — no real product or
  organisation is described. Generated by `scripts/generate_corpus.py` into
  `corpus/`, committed so the zip deliverable is self-contained. Regeneration is
  byte-identical, keeping content hashes stable for the ingestion manifest
- **Batch 1 (5 documents) is done** — coverage matrix and known gaps in
  `corpus/README.md`
- Digital-native — no scans. Scanned-document handling is a documented
  limitation, not a supported case
- **Deliberately hard**, to avoid the corpus becoming an exam we set ourselves:
  - multi-column and mixed-layout pages
  - tables spanning page breaks, and unruled tables as well as ruled
  - charts and diagrams that exist **only as images**, with information found
    nowhere in the surrounding text
  - most figures with a visible caption; a deliberate portion with none, so the
    generated-caption path is exercised
  - documents long enough to stress chunking and section context

### Design target

The ingestion/indexing/retrieval design should scale well beyond 65 documents.
That is for showcasing production-aware thinking, not because the PoC needs it.

=== CONSIDERATIONS ===
## Document corpus

### Generating the corpus — the self-set-exam trap

We author both the corpus and the pipeline that reads it. The obvious failure
mode is generating documents that happen to be easy for our own parser, which
makes the pipeline look good and proves nothing. The brief explicitly asks for
"mixed layouts" and "embedded information that requires careful processing", so
the corpus has to be *deliberately* hard — multi-column pages, unruled tables,
tables spanning page breaks, charts that exist only as images, and a portion of
figures with no visible caption so the generated-caption path actually fires.
The full specification is in requirements §4.

The same principle applies to the golden Q&A set: questions whose answers live
only in a table or only in a chart image are the ones that prove the pipeline
works.

## Document parsing

  "new_string": "The same principle applies to the golden Q&A set: questions whose answers live\nonly in a table or only in a chart image are the ones that prove the pipeline\nworks.\n\n### Corpus size \u2014 set by measurability, not by a round number\n\nThe original target was 65 documents. That figure was self-imposed: the brief\nsays nothing about corpus size, and grades architectural reasoning rather than\nvolume. It has been cut to **~25**.\n\nThe floor is not arbitrary. With five documents, recall@5 is 100% by\nconstruction \u2014 the golden set cannot distinguish a good retriever from a bad\none, so every retrieval decision (hybrid weighting, RRF, neighbour expansion,\nwhether the rewrite branches help) becomes unmeasurable. Retrieval only starts\ndiscriminating once there is enough plausible, topically adjacent material for\nthe wrong chunk to win. That is what sets the number, and ~25 clears it.\n\nThe ceiling is opportunity cost. Documents 26\u201365 would add scale but no new\ncapability: coverage of the hard aspects is complete after the first batch, and\nthe effort is better spent on the eval harness and ingestion, which are what the\ncorpus exists to serve.\n\n**This splits the corpus into two jobs with two standards.** The coverage\ndocuments are hand-authored, because a question whose answer sits only in a chart\nhas to be written deliberately. The rest are **distractors** \u2014 their job is to be\nplausible enough to compete in the ranking, which is a much lower bar than being\ninteresting. Those are authored as content data and rendered by a shared\nrenderer, with several structural variants: forty documents from one renderer\nwould share a single layout fingerprint, and a corpus that uniform quietly\nflatters the parser \u2014 the self-set-exam trap again, one level up."
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/markdown_kit.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Content-as-data pipeline: Markdown in, PDF/DOCX/HTML out.\n\nDistractor documents are authored as Markdown under `corpus/content/` and\nrendered by this module. Content stays reviewable and editable; layout lives\nhere.\n\nSupported syntax \u2014 deliberately a small subset:\n\n    ---                     front matter: title, lang, format, layout\n    # / ## / ###            headings\n    - item                  bullet list\n    | a | b |               pipe table (preceded by `{.unruled}` for a borderless one)\n    ![caption](path)        figure; empty caption renders uncaptioned\n    ::: two-column          two-column block, closed by :::\n\n`layout` picks one of several visual treatments, so the bulk of the corpus does\nnot share a single layout fingerprint.\n\"\"\"\nimport re\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nTABLE_ROW = re.compile(r\"^\\|(.+)\\|\\s*$\")\nTABLE_DIVIDER = re.compile(r\"^\\|[\\s:|-]+\\|\\s*$\")\nFIGURE = re.compile(r\"^!\\[(.*)\\]\\((.+)\\)\\s*$\")\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Block:\n    kind: str  # heading | para | bullets | table | figure | columns\n    text: str = \"\"\n    level: int = 1\n    items: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)\n    header: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)\n    rows: list[list[str]] = field(default_factory=list)\n    ruled: bool = True\n    caption: str | None = None\n    blocks: list[\"Block\"] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Doc:\n    meta: dict[str, str]\n    blocks: list[Block]\n\n    @property\n    def title(self) -> str:\n        return self.meta.get(\"title\", \"Untitled\")\n\n    @property\n    def lang(self) -> str:\n        return self.meta.get(\"lang\", \"en\")\n\n    @property\n    def fmt(self) -> str:\n        return self.meta.get(\"format\", \"html\")\n\n    @property\n    def layout(self) -> str:\n        return self.meta.get(\"layout\", \"report\")\n\n\ndef _split_front_matter(text: str) -> tuple[dict[str, str], list[str]]:\n    lines = text.splitlines()\n    if not lines or lines[0].strip() != \"---\":\n        return {}, lines\n    end = lines.index(\"---\", 1)\n    meta = {}\n    for line in lines[1:end]:\n        if \":\" in line:\n            key, _, value = line.partition(\":\")\n            meta[key.strip()] = value.strip()\n    return meta, lines[end + 1 :]\n\n\ndef _cells(line: str) -> list[str]:\n    return [cell.strip() for cell in TABLE_ROW.match(line).group(1).split(\"|\")]\n\n\ndef _parse_blocks(lines: list[str]) -> list[Block]:\n    blocks: list[Block] = []\n    index = 0\n    unruled_next = False\n\n    while index < len(lines):\n        line = lines[index]\n        stripped = line.strip()\n\n        if not stripped:\n            index += 1\n            continue\n\n        if stripped == \"{.unruled}\":\n            unruled_next = True\n            index += 1\n            continue\n\n        if stripped.startswith(\"::: two-column\"):\n            index += 1\n            nested: list[str] = []\n            while index < len(lines) and lines[index].strip() != \":::\":\n                nested.append(lines[index])\n                index += 1\n            index += 1  # closing :::\n            blocks.append(Block(kind=\"columns\", blocks=_parse_blocks(nested)))\n            continue\n\n        if stripped.startswith(\"#\"):\n            level = len(stripped) - len(stripped.lstrip(\"#\"))\n            blocks.append(Block(kind=\"heading\", level=level, text=stripped[level:].strip()))\n            index += 1\n            continue\n\n        figure = FIGURE.match(stripped)\n        if figure:\n            caption = figure.group(1).strip()\n            blocks.append(\n                Block(kind=\"figure\", text=figure.group(2), caption=caption or None)\n            )\n            index += 1\n            continue\n\n        if TABLE_ROW.match(stripped):\n            header = _cells(stripped)\n            index += 1\n            if index < len(lines) and TABLE_DIVIDER.match(lines[index].strip()):\n                index += 1\n            rows = []\n            while index < len(lines) and TABLE_ROW.match(lines[index].strip()):\n                rows.append(_cells(lines[index].strip()))\n                index += 1\n            blocks.append(Block(kind=\"table\", header=header, rows=rows, ruled=not unruled_next))\n            unruled_next = False\n            continue\n\n        if stripped.startswith(\"- \"):\n            items = []\n            while index < len(lines) and lines[index].strip().startswith(\"- \"):\n                items.append(lines[index].strip()[2:])\n                index += 1\n            blocks.append(Block(kind=\"bullets\", items=items))\n            continue\n\n        paragraph = [stripped]\n        index += 1\n        while index < len(lines) and lines[index].strip() and not _starts_block(lines[index]):\n            paragraph.append(lines[index].strip())\n            index += 1\n        blocks.append(Block(kind=\"para\", text=\" \".join(paragraph)))\n\n    return blocks\n\n\ndef _starts_block(line: str) -> bool:\n    stripped = line.strip()\n    return bool(\n        stripped.startswith((\"#\", \"- \", \":::\", \"{.unruled}\"))\n        or TABLE_ROW.match(stripped)\n        or FIGURE.match(stripped)\n    )\n\n\ndef parse(path: Path) -> Doc:\n    meta, lines = _split_front_matter(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    return Doc(meta=meta, blocks=_parse_blocks(lines))\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/sebi/workspace/sebi/chat-bot/scripts/corpus/renderers.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Render a parsed Markdown `Doc` to PDF, DOCX or HTML.\n\n`layout` in the front matter selects a visual treatment. The variants exist so\nthe bulk of the corpus does not share one structural fingerprint \u2014 a uniform\ncorpus quietly flatters the parser.\n\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom docx.shared import Pt\nfrom reportlab.lib.enums import TA_JUSTIFY, TA_LEFT\nfrom reportlab.platypus import ListFlowable, ListItem, NextPageTemplate, PageBreak, Paragraph, Spacer\n\nfrom scripts.corpus import docx_kit, markdown_kit, pdf_kit\n\nDOCS_DIR = Path(\"corpus/docs\")\n\n# font, body alignment, body size, leading, heading scale\nPDF_LAYOUTS = {\n    \"report\": (\"Helvetica\", TA_JUSTIFY, 10.0, 14.5, 1.0),\n    \"manual\": (\"Times-Roman\", TA_LEFT, 10.5, 15.0, 1.08),\n    \"brief\": (\"Helvetica\", TA_LEFT, 9.5, 13.0, 0.92),\n}\n\nHTML_LAYOUTS = {\n    \"report\": (\"Georgia, serif\", \"52rem\", \"#1b1b1b\"),\n    \"manual\": (\"'Helvetica Neue', Arial, sans-serif\", \"46rem\", \"#222222\"),\n    \"brief\": (\"'Iowan Old Style', Georgia, serif\", \"40rem\", \"#2b2b2b\"),\n}\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------- PDF ---\ndef _pdf_styles(layout: str) -> dict:\n    style = pdf_kit.styles()\n    font, alignment, size, leading, scale = PDF_LAYOUTS.get(layout, PDF_LAYOUTS[\"report\"])\n    style[\"body\"].fontName = font\n    style[\"body\"].alignment = alignment\n    style[\"body\"].fontSize = size\n    style[\"body\"].leading = leading\n    style[\"bullet\"].fontName = font\n    for name, base in ((\"h1\", 15), (\"h2\", 12.5)):\n        style[name].fontSize = base * scale\n        style[name].leading = base * scale * 1.25\n    return style\n\n\ndef _pdf_blocks(blocks: list[markdown_kit.Block], style: dict, width: float) -> list:\n    story: list = []\n    for block in blocks:\n        if block.kind == \"heading\":\n            story.append(Paragraph(block.text, style[\"h1\" if block.level <= 1 else \"h2\"]))\n        elif block.kind == \"para\":\n            story.append(Paragraph(block.text, style[\"body\"]))\n        elif block.kind == \"bullets\":\n            story.append(\n                ListFlowable(\n                    [ListItem(Paragraph(item, style[\"bullet\"])) for item in block.items],\n                    bulletType=\"bullet\",\n                    start=\"\u2022\",\n                )\n            )\n            story.append(Spacer(1, 6))\n        elif block.kind == \"table\":\n            data = [block.header, *block.rows]\n            widths = [width / len(block.header)] * len(block.header)\n            table = pdf_kit.ruled_table(data, widths) if block.ruled else pdf_kit.unruled_table(data, widths)\n            story.append(table)\n            story.append(Spacer(1, 10))\n        elif block.kind == \"figure\":\n            story += pdf_kit.figure(DOCS_DIR / block.text, width * 0.8, block.caption, style[\"caption\"])\n        elif block.kind == \"columns\":\n            # Column changes apply per page in ReportLab, so the block is fenced\n            # by page breaks.\n            story.append(NextPageTemplate(\"TwoCol\"))\n            story.append(PageBreak())\n            story += _pdf_blocks(block.blocks, style, width / 2)\n            story.append(NextPageTemplate(\"OneCol\"))\n            story.append(PageBreak())\n    return story\n\n\ndef render_pdf(doc: markdown_kit.Doc, path: Path) -> Path:\n    def story(_default_styles: dict, usable: float) -> list:\n        style = _pdf_styles(doc.layout)\n        head = [Paragraph(doc.title, style[\"title\"])]\n        if doc.meta.get(\"subtitle\"):\n            head.append(Paragraph(doc.meta[\"subtitle\"], style[\"subtitle\"]))\n        return head + _pdf_blocks(doc.blocks, style, usable)\n\n    return pdf_kit.build_pdf(path, doc.title, \"Aurora Home\", story)\n\n\n# -------------------------------------------------------------------- DOCX ---\ndef _docx_blocks(document, blocks: list[markdown_kit.Block]) -> None:\n    for block in blocks:\n        if block.kind == \"heading\":\n            document.add_heading(block.text, level=min(block.level, 4))\n        elif block.kind == \"para\":\n            document.add_paragraph(block.text)\n        elif block.kind == \"bullets\":\n            for item in block.items:\n                document.add_paragraph(item, style=\"List Bullet\")\n        elif block.kind == \"table\":\n            table = docx_kit.add_table(document, block.header, block.rows)\n            if not block.ruled:\n                table.style = \"Normal Table\"\n        elif block.kind == \"figure\":\n            docx_kit.add_figure(document, DOCS_DIR / block.text, block.caption)\n        elif block.kind == \"columns\":\n            # No column support here: DOCX columns are a section property, and a\n            # mid-document section break is not worth the complexity.\n            _docx_blocks(document, block.blocks)\n\n\ndef render_docx(doc: markdown_kit.Doc, path: Path) -> Path:\n    document = docx_kit.new_document(doc.title, doc.meta.get(\"subtitle\", \"\"))\n    if doc.layout == \"manual\":\n        style = document.styles[\"Normal\"]\n        style.font.name = \"Times New Roman\"\n        style.font.size = Pt(11)\n    elif doc.layout == \"brief\":\n        document.styles[\"Normal\"].font.size = Pt(9.5)\n    _docx_blocks(document, doc.blocks)\n    return docx_kit.save(document, path)\n\n\n# -------------------------------------------------------------------- HTML ---\ndef _escape(text: str) -> str:\n    return text.replace(\"&\", \"&amp;\").replace(\"<\", \"&lt;\").replace(\">\", \"&gt;\")\n\n\ndef _html_blocks(blocks: list[markdown_kit.Block]) -> list[str]:\n    out: list[str] = []\n    for block in blocks:\n        if block.kind == \"heading\":\n            level = min(block.level + 1, 4)\n            out.append(f\"<h{level}>{_escape(block.text)}</h{level}>\")\n        elif block.kind == \"para\":\n            out.append(f\"<p>{_escape(block.text)}</p>\")\n        elif block.kind == \"bullets\":\n            items = \"\".join(f\"<li>{_escape(item)}</li>\" for item in block.items)\n            out.append(f\"<ul>{items}</ul>\")\n        elif block.kind == \"table\":\n            head = \"\".join(f\"<th>{_escape(cell)}</th>\" for cell in block.header)\n            body = \"\".join(\n                \"<tr>\" + \"\".join(f\"<td>{_escape(cell)}</td>\" for cell in row) + \"</tr>\"\n                for row in block.rows\n            )\n            css = \"\" if block.ruled else ' class=\"unruled\"'\n            out.append(f\"<table{css}><thead><tr>{head}</tr></thead><tbody>{body}</tbody></table>\")\n        elif block.kind == \"figure\":\n            caption = f\"<figcaption>{_escape(block.caption)}</figcaption>\" if block.caption else \"\"\n            out.append(f'<figure><img src=\"{block.text}\" alt=\"\" />{caption}</figure>')\n        elif block.kind == \"columns\":\n            out.append('<div class=\"two-column\">')\n            out += _html_blocks(block.blocks)\n            out.append(\"</div>\")\n    return out\n\n\ndef render_html(doc: markdown_kit.Doc, path: Path) -> Path:\n    font, measure, ink = HTML_LAYOUTS.get(doc.layout, HTML_LAYOUTS[\"report\"])\n    subtitle = f'<p class=\"lede\">{_escape(doc.meta[\"subtitle\"])}</p>' if doc.meta.get(\"subtitle\") else \"\"\n    body = \"\\n    \".join(_html_blocks(doc.blocks))\n    html = f\"\"\"<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"{doc.lang}\">\n  <head>\n    <meta charset=\"utf-8\" />\n    <title>{_escape(doc.title)}</title>\n    <style>\n      body {{ font-family: {font}; max-width: {measure}; margin: 2rem auto; color: {ink}; }}\n      h1 {{ font-size: 1.8rem; }}\n      h2 {{ font-size: 1.25rem; margin-top: 1.8rem; border-bottom: 1px solid #ddd; }}\n      h3 {{ font-size: 1.05rem; }}\n      .lede {{ color: #555; font-style: italic; }}\n      .two-column {{ column-count: 2; column-gap: 2.5rem; }}\n      figure img {{ width: 100%; max-width: 32rem; }}\n      table {{ border-collapse: collapse; margin: 1rem 0; }}\n      th, td {{ border: 1px solid #999; padding: .3rem .55rem; text-align: left; }}\n      th {{ background: #e8e3d8; }}\n      table.unruled th, table.unruled td {{ border: none; padding: .2rem 1.2rem .2rem 0; }}\n    </style>\n  </head>\n  <body>\n    <h1>{_escape(doc.title)}</h1>\n    {subtitle}\n    {body}\n  </body>\n</html>\n\"\"\"\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    path.write_text(html, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    return path\n\n\nRENDERERS = {\"pdf\": render_pdf, \"docx\": render_docx, \"html\": render_html}\n\n\ndef render(doc: markdown_kit.Doc, out_dir: Path, stem: str) -> Path:\n    renderer = RENDERERS[doc.fmt]\n    return renderer(doc, out_dir / f\"{stem}.{doc.fmt}\")\n"
}
